The biggest part is definitely trying to stop something bad from happening. Like, even after I know I did the safety check, my mind keeps hitting me with, “What if you missed something on the cab?” And then it goes straight to somebody getting hurt because I didn’t catch it. That’s the part that scares me the most.\n\nBut it also turns into what people would think, especially my boss. I get stuck on this idea that if something went wrong, he’d say I lied — like I claimed I checked it but didn’t really do it right. And I know that sounds kind of specific, but it just repeats in my head. It’s not like I can think, “Okay, I checked it, I’m done.” It keeps coming back while I’m driving, or when I’m trying to study, and I’ll be sitting there trying to focus but my brain is still back at the cab, replaying whether I missed something.\n\nSo yeah… it’s mainly about preventing someone from getting hurt, but then right behind that is this fear that people will think I was careless or dishonest. I can’t really make either part shut up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about missing something in your safety checks are a part of you, or do they feel like they're coming from outside of your own thoughts?\n\nPatient: They feel like they’re coming from my own head, I guess… not like a voice outside me or like somebody’s putting them there. It’s more like my own thoughts turn against me, if that makes sense.\n\nLike I’ll know I did the safety check. I can even remember doing it. But then this thought keeps hitting me anyway, like, “What if you missed something on the cab?” And it doesn’t feel like I chose to think that. It just pops back in, over and over, and then I’m stuck thinking somebody could get hurt or my boss could say I lied about checking it.\n\nSo it’s part of my thoughts, but it doesn’t feel like the normal kind of thinking where I can just decide to stop. It’s in my own mind, but it feels unwanted and it won’t shut up, especially when I’m driving or trying to study.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about missing something in your safety checks are something you want to think about, or do they feel unwanted?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t want to think about them at all. That’s kind of the whole problem. I’ll do the safety check and I’ll know, like logically, that I did it, but then the thought just comes right back anyway — like, what if I missed something on the cab, what if there’s something wrong and somebody gets hurt because of me.\n\nIt feels really unwanted, like it keeps forcing itself back into my head. I’m not sitting there trying to worry about it or analyze it on purpose. I’ll be driving and I need to be paying attention, or I’ll be trying to study, and it’s still there, replaying the check or making me doubt whether I really did it right. And then it turns into, what if my boss thinks I lied and says I didn’t actually check it properly.\n\nSo yeah, it’s definitely not something I want. I’ve tried to just tell myself, “You checked it, stop thinking about it,” but it doesn’t really shut up.\n\nDoctor: How distressed do you feel when these unwanted thoughts about missing something in your safety checks occur?\n\nPatient: Really distressed. Like… it doesn’t just feel like a normal worry where I can go, “Okay, I checked it, move on.” It hits me hard because the thought is always that I missed something on the cab and somebody could get hurt because of me. And even if part of me knows I did the safety check, the feeling doesn’t match that. It’s like my chest tightens and my brain just keeps circling back to it.\n\nAnd then I start thinking, what if my boss says I lied, like I said I checked it but I didn’t really do it right. That part makes it worse, because then it’s not just someone getting hurt, it’s also me being seen as careless or dishonest. I can’t really calm down from it easily.\n\nIt happens while I’m driving, which is awful because I’m supposed to be focused, and it happens when I’m trying to study too. I’ll be sitting there trying to read something and I’m still mentally back at the cab, replaying the safety check. So yeah, it’s very distressing. It feels like I can’t make it shut up, even when I know I already checked.\n\nDoctor: Are your repeated safety checks on the cab primarily done to prevent a specific bad event from happening?\n\nPatient: Yes… mostly, yeah. In my head it’s like I’m trying to stop something bad from happening before I take a ride. Like before I can actually leave, I keep going around the cab checking everything — the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, the paperwork — and I don’t just check once. I’ll go around again and again because it doesn’t feel settled.\n\nSometimes I’ll even take pictures, like to prove to myself I checked it, but then I’ll look at the picture and still think, “What if I missed something outside the frame?” or “What if I checked it wrong?” And then if it doesn’t feel right, I restart the whole check from the beginning, which is really frustrating because passengers can be waiting and I’m already late.\n\nSo yeah, it’s not just checking to be careful. It feels like if I don’t do it right, something could go wrong during the ride, and it would be my fault.\n\nDoctor: Are there any areas of your life where you feel like these thoughts and checking rituals are holding you back or causing problems that you hadn't mentioned yet?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s definitely causing problems in a lot of places now. Work is the biggest one, I guess, because if I can’t finish checking the cab and feel okay enough to leave, I just don’t get on the road on time. And then I’ve lost rides because of that. Like I’ll be supposed to pick someone up or start taking fares, and I’m still standing there going around the car again, or looking back at something I already checked, and by the time I’m ready it’s too late.\n\nI’ve gotten warnings for it too, because from the outside it probably just looks like I’m being slow or not managing my time, but it’s not like I’m just messing around. I’m stuck. I know I need to go, and I’m watching the time, and that makes me panic more, but then the checking gets worse because I’m thinking, “If I rush, that’s when I’ll miss something.” So it kind of traps me.\n\nAnd my CDL studying keeps getting pushed back. I’m trying to study and move forward, but so much of my day gets eaten up by the checking and then being behind afterward. By the time I actually sit down to study, I’m tired or I’m still thinking about whether I checked something right earlier. So it’s not just five minutes here and there — it takes over way more of the day than I want to admit.\n\nI barely see my friends anymore too. Not because I don’t want to, but because I’m either behind on work from the delays, or I’m trying to catch up on studying or other stuff I didn’t get done because I lost all that time checking. And then if someone asks me to hang out, I’m like, “I can’t, I’m behind,” but I’m always behind now. It’s embarrassing. It feels like my whole schedule is being controlled by this one thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts and behaviors are a part of who you are, or do they feel alien to you?\n\nPatient: They feel alien to me. I mean… it’s weird, because it’s my own brain saying it, so in that sense it feels like it’s coming from me, but it doesn’t feel like who I am or what I want to be thinking about all day.\n\nLike, I know I did the safety check. I can remember doing it. But then this thought keeps hitting me anyway, like, “What if you missed something on the cab?” or “What if somebody gets hurt because you didn’t catch it?” And then it turns into, “What if your boss thinks you lied when you said you checked it?” That part really bothers me, because I don’t want to be careless, and I don’t want anyone thinking I’m dishonest.\n\nSo no, it doesn’t feel like a part of my personality or something I believe in. It feels more like something attacking me, honestly. I’ll be driving or trying to study, and it just keeps coming back when I’m trying to focus on what I’m actually supposed to be doing. I can’t make it shut up, even when another part of me is like, “You already checked. You know you checked.”\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about the safety checks are in line with your own values and beliefs?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my values are basically the opposite, I think. I do care about safety, obviously, and I don’t want anybody getting hurt in my cab. I want to be honest with my boss and do the check properly. That part is me.\n\nBut the thoughts themselves don’t feel like they’re “in line” with me. They feel like they take that normal thing — like wanting to be safe — and twist it until it’s all I can think about. Even when I know I did the safety check, this thought just keeps hitting me, like, “You missed something,” or “Somebody could get hurt because of you,” or “Your boss is going to think you lied about checking it.” And I can tell myself, “No, I checked it,” but it doesn’t really shut it up.\n\nIt’s especially bad because it follows me after the check too. Like I’ll be driving or trying to study, and instead of focusing, my mind keeps going back to the cab and whether I missed something. That’s not how I want to be. I don’t want to be stuck replaying the same fear over and over. So… no, I’d say the safety part lines up with my values, but the thoughts and the way they keep attacking me don’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about the safety checks are something you would normally think about, or do they feel out of character for you?\n\nPatient: No, it feels really out of character for me. I mean, I would normally care about doing the safety check right, because that’s part of the job and I don’t want to be irresponsible. But this is different. It’s like even after I know I did it, and I can remember doing it, the thought still comes back hard, like, “What if you missed something on the cab?” or “What if somebody gets hurt because of you?” And then it goes to, “What if your boss thinks you lied when you said you checked it?”\n\nThat’s not how I normally think. I don’t usually sit there doubting myself over and over like that. Or at least I didn’t used to. It feels like my brain is grabbing onto this one thing and won’t let go. I’ll be driving or trying to study, and I’m not even trying to think about the cab anymore, but it just pops back in and I can’t make it shut up.\n\nSo yeah, it feels out of character. The caring about safety part is normal for me, but the way the thought keeps attacking me after I’ve already checked… that doesn’t feel like me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to repeat any specific actions or rituals in response to these intrusive thoughts about missing something in your safety checks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. That’s probably the part that’s been getting the most out of control.\n\nBefore I can take a ride, I don’t just do the check once and move on. I’ll go around the car and check the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, the paperwork… and then I’ll finish, and almost immediately it feels like I have to do it again. Like if I don’t repeat it, then I’m just leaving something dangerous there on purpose, even though I know that’s not really true.\n\nSometimes I’ll go around the whole cab multiple times. I’ll check a tire, then the lights, then the doors, then I’ll think, “Wait, did I actually look closely enough at that tire?” and then I start the whole thing over. Or if I get interrupted, or if something just doesn’t feel right, I’ll restart from the beginning, because it feels like the whole check doesn’t count anymore.\n\nI’ve even taken pictures sometimes, like of the tires or certain things, just so I can prove to myself I checked it. But then even with the pictures, I’ll zoom in and look again, or I’ll think maybe the picture didn’t show the right angle, so then I’m back outside checking again.\n\nAnd it’s embarrassing because this happens when passengers are waiting too. I’ll know I’m already late, or I’ll know someone is standing there expecting me to leave, and I’m still stuck going around the car again because my head is saying, “No, you missed something, you have to make sure.” It’s like I can’t let myself start the ride until the check feels right, and a lot of the time it just doesn’t.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah… it’s definitely causing problems. It’s not just annoying or like I’m being careful. It’s actually messing up my life.

Work is the biggest one. I’ve lost rides because I can’t get on the road on time. Like I’ll have someone waiting or I’ll be supposed to start, and I’m still stuck doing the checks again, and by the time I’m finally ready, the ride is gone or I’m late enough that it becomes a problem. I’ve gotten warnings for it too, which is embarrassing, because I’m not trying to be lazy or avoid work. I’m trying to do the job right, but it ends up looking like I can’t manage myself.

And then it eats up so much of the day. It’s like I think, “Okay, I’ll just check and go,” but then the checking takes way longer than it should, and everything after that gets pushed back. I’m supposed to be studying for my CDL, and I keep telling myself I’ll do it after work or when I get home, but by then I’m either behind because of all the delays, or I’m exhausted from trying to catch up. So the studying just keeps getting pushed back and pushed back.

And friends… yeah, I barely see them anymore. Not because I don’t want to, but because I’m always either behind on work, or trying to make up for lost time, or just stuck dealing with the fallout from being delayed. If someone asks me to hang out, I’m usually thinking, “I can’t, I’m already behind,” or I feel like I don’t have time because so much of the day got swallowed by the checks.

So yeah, it’s causing a lot of stress. It feels like this one thing is taking over way more of my life than it should.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF42.0, F42.1, F42.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I can’t really get this thought to stop. Like, even when I know I did the safety check on my cab — I’ll remember doing it, or at least I think I remember doing it — this thought just hits me that maybe I missed something. Like maybe there’s something wrong with the cab and someone could get hurt, or my boss will think I lied about checking it.\n\nAnd it’s not just a quick worry and then it goes away. It keeps coming back while I’m driving, and then I’m trying to focus on the road but in my head I’m going over it again and again, like, “Did I really check that? What if I skipped something?” It makes it hard to drive normally, and even when I’m trying to study it’s there too, like I can’t shut it up. It’s exhausting, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel the need to perform specific actions or rituals to try and get rid of these thoughts about potentially missing something in your safety checks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I do. It’s kind of embarrassing because it starts as a normal safety check, like what I’m supposed to do before taking a ride, but then I can’t stop at just doing it once.\n\nI’ll go around the cab and check the tires, then the lights, then the doors, then the meter, then the paperwork, and I’ll think, “Okay, that’s done.” But then as soon as I’m about to let someone in or start driving, I get this feeling like it didn’t count, or like maybe I only looked but didn’t really check properly. So I go around again. And sometimes I’ll take pictures of things, like to prove to myself I checked them, but then even the pictures don’t always help because I’ll think maybe the picture was from before or maybe I didn’t check the right angle or something.\n\nAnd if it doesn’t feel right — like if I get interrupted, or I’m not completely sure in my head — I’ll restart the whole thing from the beginning. Tires, lights, doors, meter, paperwork, all over again. It can happen even when passengers are standing there waiting, which makes me feel terrible, and sometimes I’m already late but I still can’t just leave it. It’s like I need the check to feel “complete,” not just be complete.\n\nDoctor: Are these repeated checks you're doing intended to prevent something bad from happening, like an accident?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s what it feels like. Like I know the checks are supposed to prevent something bad, but I take it way past the normal amount. Before I can take a ride, I keep going around the car checking the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, the paperwork — and then even after I’ve done all of that, I’ll think, “What if I missed one thing and someone gets hurt?”\n\nSo I do it again. And sometimes again. And if it doesn’t feel right in my head, like if I wasn’t paying attention perfectly or someone distracted me, I’ll start the whole check over from the beginning. I’ve even taken pictures to try to prove to myself I checked, but then I start doubting that too.\n\nAnd it happens even when passengers are waiting there and I’m already late, which makes me feel awful, but I still feel like I can’t just drive off unless I’m sure. It’s like I’m trying to stop an accident or stop myself from being blamed for one, even if part of me knows I already checked.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that you fear might happen if you don't complete these checks perfectly?\n\nPatient: Yeah… the main thing is that I’ll miss something on the cab and then somebody gets hurt because of it. Like maybe I didn’t really check a tire properly, or a light, or something with the doors, and then I drive off and something happens. Even if I remember doing the check, there’s this thought that comes in like, “But what if you only think you did it? What if you skipped the important part?”\n\nAnd then there’s also this fear that if something did happen, my boss would say I lied about checking it, or that I was careless. Like I’d be responsible because I didn’t do it perfectly enough. That’s the part that keeps replaying in my head while I’m driving. I’m supposed to be focused, obviously, but instead I’m thinking back over the check again and again, trying to make sure I didn’t miss anything.\n\nIt’s not like I want to think about it. I know I did the check, or at least I’ll have this memory of doing it, but it doesn’t settle. The fear is basically that if I don’t get it completely right, someone could get hurt and it would be my fault, or people would think I lied about doing my job.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts and checking rituals affecting your relationships with friends, family, or colleagues?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has, definitely. Mostly with work, honestly, but that kind of spills into everything else. I’ve lost rides because I couldn’t get on the road fast enough. Like someone will be waiting, or I’m supposed to be ready, and I’m still going around the cab checking things over and over, and by the time I’m done, the ride is gone or they’re upset. I’ve gotten warnings for being late or not moving when I’m supposed to, which is… humiliating, because I’m not trying to be lazy or careless. It’s actually the opposite, but it doesn’t look that way from the outside.\n\nWith colleagues, it’s awkward because people notice. They’ll be like, “Are you still checking?” or they’ll get annoyed if I’m holding things up. I don’t really know how to explain it without sounding ridiculous, so I usually just say I’m making sure everything’s okay, but I can tell they think I’m overdoing it.\n\nAnd friends… I barely see them now. It’s not like I don’t want to. It’s just that so much of my day gets eaten up by the checking, and then I’m behind on work, and then I’m trying to catch up afterward. So if someone asks me to hang out, I’m either late, exhausted, or I feel like I can’t because I still have things I was supposed to do.\n\nEven my studying for my CDL keeps getting pushed back. I keep telling myself I’ll study after work, but then the day gets messed up from all the delays, and by the time I get home I’m just trying to recover or catch up. So yeah, it’s affecting a lot more than just the actual checking. It’s like everything gets backed up behind it.\n\nDoctor: Are you having these repetitive, intrusive thoughts about anything else besides the safety checks on your cab?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s that same cab safety thing. Like, it follows me around more than I’d expect. It’s not just when I’m standing there doing the check — it keeps coming back after I’ve already finished, even when I know I did it.\n\nI’ll be driving and this thought just hits me like, “What if you missed something? What if a tire wasn’t right, or the door wasn’t secure, and somebody gets hurt?” And then it turns into, “What if my boss thinks I lied about checking it?” or that I said I did it but didn’t really do it properly. I keep replaying it in my head, trying to prove to myself that I did everything, but it doesn’t really shut off.\n\nAnd it happens when I’m trying to study too. Like I’ll sit down and try to focus, and my mind goes right back to the cab — did I check this, did I miss that, what if something happens because of me. So… besides the safety checks, not really some whole separate topic, but the thoughts about the checks keep intruding into everything else.\n\nDoctor: Are you having these kinds of intrusive thoughts about anything else in your life besides the cab safety checks?\n\nPatient: Not really anything totally separate, no. It’s mainly the cab checks, but it doesn’t stay at the cab, if that makes sense. Like even when I know I did the safety check, the thought keeps coming back anyway — that I missed something, or I only think I checked it, and then somebody could get hurt because of me.\n\nAnd then it kind of branches into this thing where I picture my boss saying I lied, like I said I checked it but I didn’t really do it right. That part really gets stuck in my head. I’ll be driving and trying to focus on the road, and it’s still there in the background, like, “What if you missed it? What if something happens?” Or I’ll try to study, and instead of paying attention I’m going back over the check in my mind again.\n\nSo it’s not like I have a bunch of different fears about different parts of my life. It’s mostly that one thing, but it follows me into everything and I can’t really make it shut up.\n\nDoctor: Are your intrusive thoughts more about preventing something bad from happening, or are they also about what others might think of you if something goes wrong?\n\nPatient: It’s both, honestly. The biggest part is definitely trying to stop something bad from happening. Like, even after I know I did the safety check, my mind keeps hitting me with, “What if you missed something on the cab?” And then it goes straight to somebody getting hurt because I didn’t catch it. That’s the part that scares me the most.\n\nBut it also turns into what people would think, especially my boss. I get stuck on this idea that if something went wrong, he’d say I lied — like I claimed I checked it but didn’t really do it right. And I know that sounds kind of specific, but it just repeats in my head. It’s not like I can think, “Okay, I checked it, I’m done.” It keeps coming back while I’m driving, or when I’m trying to study, and I’ll be sitting there trying to focus but my brain is still back at the cab, replaying whether I missed something.\n\nSo yeah… it’s mainly about preventing someone from getting hurt, but then right behind that is this fear that people will think I was careless or dishonest. I can’t really make either part shut up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about missing something in your safety checks are a part of you, or do they feel like they're coming from outside of your own thoughts?\n\nPatient: They feel like they’re coming from my own head, I guess… not like a voice outside me or like somebody’s putting them there. It’s more like my own thoughts turn against me, if that makes sense.\n\nLike I’ll know I did the safety check. I can even remember doing it. But then this thought keeps hitting me anyway, like, “What if you missed something on the cab?” And it doesn’t feel like I chose to think that. It just pops back in, over and over, and then I’m stuck thinking somebody could get hurt or my boss could say I lied about checking it.\n\nSo it’s part of my thoughts, but it doesn’t feel like the normal kind of thinking where I can just decide to stop. It’s in my own mind, but it feels unwanted and it won’t shut up, especially when I’m driving or trying to study.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about missing something in your safety checks are something you want to think about, or do they feel unwanted?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t want to think about them at all. That’s kind of the whole problem. I’ll do the safety check and I’ll know, like logically, that I did it, but then the thought just comes right back anyway — like, what if I missed something on the cab, what if there’s something wrong and somebody gets hurt because of me.\n\nIt feels really unwanted, like it keeps forcing itself back into my head. I’m not sitting there trying to worry about it or analyze it on purpose. I’ll be driving and I need to be paying attention, or I’ll be trying to study, and it’s still there, replaying the check or making me doubt whether I really did it right. And then it turns into, what if my boss thinks I lied and says I didn’t actually check it properly.\n\nSo yeah, it’s definitely not something I want. I’ve tried to just tell myself, “You checked it, stop thinking about it,” but it doesn’t really shut up.\n\nDoctor: How distressed do you feel when these unwanted thoughts about missing something in your safety checks occur?\n\nPatient: Really distressed. Like… it doesn’t just feel like a normal worry where I can go, “Okay, I checked it, move on.” It hits me hard because the thought is always that I missed something on the cab and somebody could get hurt because of me. And even if part of me knows I did the safety check, the feeling doesn’t match that. It’s like my chest tightens and my brain just keeps circling back to it.\n\nAnd then I start thinking, what if my boss says I lied, like I said I checked it but I didn’t really do it right. That part makes it worse, because then it’s not just someone getting hurt, it’s also me being seen as careless or dishonest. I can’t really calm down from it easily.\n\nIt happens while I’m driving, which is awful because I’m supposed to be focused, and it happens when I’m trying to study too. I’ll be sitting there trying to read something and I’m still mentally back at the cab, replaying the safety check. So yeah, it’s very distressing. It feels like I can’t make it shut up, even when I know I already checked.\n\nDoctor: Are your repeated safety checks on the cab primarily done to prevent a specific bad event from happening?\n\nPatient: Yes… mostly, yeah. In my head it’s like I’m trying to stop something bad from happening before I take a ride. Like before I can actually leave, I keep going around the cab checking everything — the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, the paperwork — and I don’t just check once. I’ll go around again and again because it doesn’t feel settled.\n\nSometimes I’ll even take pictures, like to prove to myself I checked it, but then I’ll look at the picture and still think, “What if I missed something outside the frame?” or “What if I checked it wrong?” And then if it doesn’t feel right, I restart the whole check from the beginning, which is really frustrating because passengers can be waiting and I’m already late.\n\nSo yeah, it’s not just checking to be careful. It feels like if I don’t do it right, something could go wrong during the ride, and it would be my fault.\n\nDoctor: Are there any areas of your life where you feel like these thoughts and checking rituals are holding you back or causing problems that you hadn't mentioned yet?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s definitely causing problems in a lot of places now. Work is the biggest one, I guess, because if I can’t finish checking the cab and feel okay enough to leave, I just don’t get on the road on time. And then I’ve lost rides because of that. Like I’ll be supposed to pick someone up or start taking fares, and I’m still standing there going around the car again, or looking back at something I already checked, and by the time I’m ready it’s too late.\n\nI’ve gotten warnings for it too, because from the outside it probably just looks like I’m being slow or not managing my time, but it’s not like I’m just messing around. I’m stuck. I know I need to go, and I’m watching the time, and that makes me panic more, but then the checking gets worse because I’m thinking, “If I rush, that’s when I’ll miss something.” So it kind of traps me.\n\nAnd my CDL studying keeps getting pushed back. I’m trying to study and move forward, but so much of my day gets eaten up by the checking and then being behind afterward. By the time I actually sit down to study, I’m tired or I’m still thinking about whether I checked something right earlier. So it’s not just five minutes here and there — it takes over way more of the day than I want to admit.\n\nI barely see my friends anymore too. Not because I don’t want to, but because I’m either behind on work from the delays, or I’m trying to catch up on studying or other stuff I didn’t get done because I lost all that time checking. And then if someone asks me to hang out, I’m like, “I can’t, I’m behind,” but I’m always behind now. It’s embarrassing. It feels like my whole schedule is being controlled by this one thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts and behaviors are a part of who you are, or do they feel alien to you?\n\nPatient: They feel alien to me. I mean… it’s weird, because it’s my own brain saying it, so in that sense it feels like it’s coming from me, but it doesn’t feel like who I am or what I want to be thinking about all day.\n\nLike, I know I did the safety check. I can remember doing it. But then this thought keeps hitting me anyway, like, “What if you missed something on the cab?” or “What if somebody gets hurt because you didn’t catch it?” And then it turns into, “What if your boss thinks you lied when you said you checked it?” That part really bothers me, because I don’t want to be careless, and I don’t want anyone thinking I’m dishonest.\n\nSo no, it doesn’t feel like a part of my personality or something I believe in. It feels more like something attacking me, honestly. I’ll be driving or trying to study, and it just keeps coming back when I’m trying to focus on what I’m actually supposed to be doing. I can’t make it shut up, even when another part of me is like, “You already checked. You know you checked.”\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about the safety checks are in line with your own values and beliefs?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my values are basically the opposite, I think. I do care about safety, obviously, and I don’t want anybody getting hurt in my cab. I want to be honest with my boss and do the check properly. That part is me.\n\nBut the thoughts themselves don’t feel like they’re “in line” with me. They feel like they take that normal thing — like wanting to be safe — and twist it until it’s all I can think about. Even when I know I did the safety check, this thought just keeps hitting me, like, “You missed something,” or “Somebody could get hurt because of you,” or “Your boss is going to think you lied about checking it.” And I can tell myself, “No, I checked it,” but it doesn’t really shut it up.\n\nIt’s especially bad because it follows me after the check too. Like I’ll be driving or trying to study, and instead of focusing, my mind keeps going back to the cab and whether I missed something. That’s not how I want to be. I don’t want to be stuck replaying the same fear over and over. So… no, I’d say the safety part lines up with my values, but the thoughts and the way they keep attacking me don’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about the safety checks are something you would normally think about, or do they feel out of character for you?\n\nPatient: No, it feels really out of character for me. I mean, I would normally care about doing the safety check right, because that’s part of the job and I don’t want to be irresponsible. But this is different. It’s like even after I know I did it, and I can remember doing it, the thought still comes back hard, like, “What if you missed something on the cab?” or “What if somebody gets hurt because of you?” And then it goes to, “What if your boss thinks you lied when you said you checked it?”\n\nThat’s not how I normally think. I don’t usually sit there doubting myself over and over like that. Or at least I didn’t used to. It feels like my brain is grabbing onto this one thing and won’t let go. I’ll be driving or trying to study, and I’m not even trying to think about the cab anymore, but it just pops back in and I can’t make it shut up.\n\nSo yeah, it feels out of character. The caring about safety part is normal for me, but the way the thought keeps attacking me after I’ve already checked… that doesn’t feel like me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to repeat any specific actions or rituals in response to these intrusive thoughts about missing something in your safety checks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. That’s probably the part that’s been getting the most out of control.\n\nBefore I can take a ride, I don’t just do the check once and move on. I’ll go around the car and check the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, the paperwork… and then I’ll finish, and almost immediately it feels like I have to do it again. Like if I don’t repeat it, then I’m just leaving something dangerous there on purpose, even though I know that’s not really true.\n\nSometimes I’ll go around the whole cab multiple times. I’ll check a tire, then the lights, then the doors, then I’ll think, “Wait, did I actually look closely enough at that tire?” and then I start the whole thing over. Or if I get interrupted, or if something just doesn’t feel right, I’ll restart from the beginning, because it feels like the whole check doesn’t count anymore.\n\nI’ve even taken pictures sometimes, like of the tires or certain things, just so I can prove to myself I checked it. But then even with the pictures, I’ll zoom in and look again, or I’ll think maybe the picture didn’t show the right angle, so then I’m back outside checking again.\n\nAnd it’s embarrassing because this happens when passengers are waiting too. I’ll know I’m already late, or I’ll know someone is standing there expecting me to leave, and I’m still stuck going around the car again because my head is saying, “No, you missed something, you have to make sure.” It’s like I can’t let myself start the ride until the check feels right, and a lot of the time it just doesn’t.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts and checking rituals causing you significant distress or problems in your daily life, such as at work, with friends, or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s definitely causing problems. It’s not just annoying or like I’m being careful. It’s actually messing up my life.\n\nWork is the biggest one. I’ve lost rides because I can’t get on the road on time. Like I’ll have someone waiting or I’ll be supposed to start, and I’m still stuck doing the checks again, and by the time I’m finally ready, the ride is gone or I’m late enough that it becomes a problem. I’ve gotten warnings for it too, which is embarrassing, because I’m not trying to be lazy or avoid work. I’m trying to do the job right, but it ends up looking like I can’t manage myself.\n\nAnd then it eats up so much of the day. It’s like I think, “Okay, I’ll just check and go,” but then the checking takes way longer than it should, and everything after that gets pushed back. I’m supposed to be studying for my CDL, and I keep telling myself I’ll do it after work or when I get home, but by then I’m either behind because of all the delays, or I’m exhausted from trying to catch up. So the studying just keeps getting pushed back and pushed back.\n\nAnd friends… yeah, I barely see them anymore. Not because I don’t want to, but because I’m always either behind on work, or trying to make up for lost time, or just stuck dealing with the fallout from being delayed. If someone asks me to hang out, I’m usually thinking, “I can’t, I’m already behind,” or I feel like I don’t have time because so much of the day got swallowed by the checks.\n\nSo yeah, it’s causing a lot of stress. It feels like this one thing is taking over way more of my life than it should.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Like, it follows me around more than I’d expect. It’s not just when I’m standing there doing the check — it keeps coming back after I’ve already finished, even when I know I did it.\n\nI’ll be driving and this thought just hits me like, “What if you missed something? What if a tire wasn’t right, or the door wasn’t secure, and somebody gets hurt?” And then it turns into, “What if my boss thinks I lied about checking it?” or that I said I did it but didn’t really do it properly. I keep replaying it in my head, trying to prove to myself that I did everything, but it doesn’t really shut off.\n\nAnd it happens when I’m trying to study too. Like I’ll sit down and try to focus, and my mind goes right back to the cab — did I check this, did I miss that, what if something happens because of me. So… besides the safety checks, not really some whole separate topic, but the thoughts about the checks keep intruding into everything else.\n\nDoctor: Are you having these kinds of intrusive thoughts about anything else in your life besides the cab safety checks?\n\nPatient: Not really anything totally separate, no. It’s mainly the cab checks, but it doesn’t stay at the cab, if that makes sense. Like even when I know I did the safety check, the thought keeps coming back anyway — that I missed something, or I only think I checked it, and then somebody could get hurt because of me.\n\nAnd then it kind of branches into this thing where I picture my boss saying I lied, like I said I checked it but I didn’t really do it right. That part really gets stuck in my head. I’ll be driving and trying to focus on the road, and it’s still there in the background, like, “What if you missed it? What if something happens?” Or I’ll try to study, and instead of paying attention I’m going back over the check in my mind again.\n\nSo it’s not like I have a bunch of different fears about different parts of my life. It’s mostly that one thing, but it follows me into everything and I can’t really make it shut up.\n\nDoctor: Are your intrusive thoughts more about preventing something bad from happening, or are they also about what others might think of you if something goes wrong?\n\nPatient: It’s both, honestly. The biggest part is definitely trying to stop something bad from happening. Like, even after I know I did the safety check, my mind keeps hitting me with, “What if you missed something on the cab?” And then it goes straight to somebody getting hurt because I didn’t catch it. That’s the part that scares me the most.\n\nBut it also turns into what people would think, especially my boss. I get stuck on this idea that if something went wrong, he’d say I lied — like I claimed I checked it but didn’t really do it right. And I know that sounds kind of specific, but it just repeats in my head. It’s not like I can think, “Okay, I checked it, I’m done.” It keeps coming back while I’m driving, or when I’m trying to study, and I’ll be sitting there trying to focus but my brain is still back at the cab, replaying whether I missed something.\n\nSo yeah… it’s mainly about preventing someone from getting hurt, but then right behind that is this fear that people will think I was careless or dishonest. I can’t really make either part shut up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about missing something in your safety checks are a part of you, or do they feel like they're coming from outside of your own thoughts?\n\nPatient: They feel like they’re coming from my own head, I guess… not like a voice outside me or like somebody’s putting them there. It’s more like my own thoughts turn against me, if that makes sense.\n\nLike I’ll know I did the safety check. I can even remember doing it. But then this thought keeps hitting me anyway, like, “What if you missed something on the cab?” And it doesn’t feel like I chose to think that. It just pops back in, over and over, and then I’m stuck thinking somebody could get hurt or my boss could say I lied about checking it.\n\nSo it’s part of my thoughts, but it doesn’t feel like the normal kind of thinking where I can just decide to stop. It’s in my own mind, but it feels unwanted and it won’t shut up, especially when I’m driving or trying to study.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about missing something in your safety checks are something you want to think about, or do they feel unwanted?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t want to think about them at all. That’s kind of the whole problem. I’ll do the safety check and I’ll know, like logically, that I did it, but then the thought just comes right back anyway — like, what if I missed something on the cab, what if there’s something wrong and somebody gets hurt because of me.\n\nIt feels really unwanted, like it keeps forcing itself back into my head. I’m not sitting there trying to worry about it or analyze it on purpose. I’ll be driving and I need to be paying attention, or I’ll be trying to study, and it’s still there, replaying the check or making me doubt whether I really did it right. And then it turns into, what if my boss thinks I lied and says I didn’t actually check it properly.\n\nSo yeah, it’s definitely not something I want. I’ve tried to just tell myself, “You checked it, stop thinking about it,” but it doesn’t really shut up.\n\nDoctor: How distressed do you feel when these unwanted thoughts about missing something in your safety checks occur?\n\nPatient: Really distressed. Like… it doesn’t just feel like a normal worry where I can go, “Okay, I checked it, move on.” It hits me hard because the thought is always that I missed something on the cab and somebody could get hurt because of me. And even if part of me knows I did the safety check, the feeling doesn’t match that. It’s like my chest tightens and my brain just keeps circling back to it.\n\nAnd then I start thinking, what if my boss says I lied, like I said I checked it but I didn’t really do it right. That part makes it worse, because then it’s not just someone getting hurt, it’s also me being seen as careless or dishonest. I can’t really calm down from it easily.\n\nIt happens while I’m driving, which is awful because I’m supposed to be focused, and it happens when I’m trying to study too. I’ll be sitting there trying to read something and I’m still mentally back at the cab, replaying the safety check. So yeah, it’s very distressing. It feels like I can’t make it shut up, even when I know I already checked.\n\nDoctor: Are your repeated safety checks on the cab primarily done to prevent a specific bad event from happening?\n\nPatient: Yes… mostly, yeah. In my head it’s like I’m trying to stop something bad from happening before I take a ride. Like before I can actually leave, I keep going around the cab checking everything — the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, the paperwork — and I don’t just check once. I’ll go around again and again because it doesn’t feel settled.\n\nSometimes I’ll even take pictures, like to prove to myself I checked it, but then I’ll look at the picture and still think, “What if I missed something outside the frame?” or “What if I checked it wrong?” And then if it doesn’t feel right, I restart the whole check from the beginning, which is really frustrating because passengers can be waiting and I’m already late.\n\nSo yeah, it’s not just checking to be careful. It feels like if I don’t do it right, something could go wrong during the ride, and it would be my fault.\n\nDoctor: Are there any areas of your life where you feel like these thoughts and checking rituals are holding you back or causing problems that you hadn't mentioned yet?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s definitely causing problems in a lot of places now. Work is the biggest one, I guess, because if I can’t finish checking the cab and feel okay enough to leave, I just don’t get on the road on time. And then I’ve lost rides because of that. Like I’ll be supposed to pick someone up or start taking fares, and I’m still standing there going around the car again, or looking back at something I already checked, and by the time I’m ready it’s too late.\n\nI’ve gotten warnings for it too, because from the outside it probably just looks like I’m being slow or not managing my time, but it’s not like I’m just messing around. I’m stuck. I know I need to go, and I’m watching the time, and that makes me panic more, but then the checking gets worse because I’m thinking, “If I rush, that’s when I’ll miss something.” So it kind of traps me.\n\nAnd my CDL studying keeps getting pushed back. I’m trying to study and move forward, but so much of my day gets eaten up by the checking and then being behind afterward. By the time I actually sit down to study, I’m tired or I’m still thinking about whether I checked something right earlier. So it’s not just five minutes here and there — it takes over way more of the day than I want to admit.\n\nI barely see my friends anymore too. Not because I don’t want to, but because I’m either behind on work from the delays, or I’m trying to catch up on studying or other stuff I didn’t get done because I lost all that time checking. And then if someone asks me to hang out, I’m like, “I can’t, I’m behind,” but I’m always behind now. It’s embarrassing. It feels like my whole schedule is being controlled by this one thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts and behaviors are a part of who you are, or do they feel alien to you?\n\nPatient: They feel alien to me. I mean… it’s weird, because it’s my own brain saying it, so in that sense it feels like it’s coming from me, but it doesn’t feel like who I am or what I want to be thinking about all day.\n\nLike, I know I did the safety check. I can remember doing it. But then this thought keeps hitting me anyway, like, “What if you missed something on the cab?” or “What if somebody gets hurt because you didn’t catch it?” And then it turns into, “What if your boss thinks you lied when you said you checked it?” That part really bothers me, because I don’t want to be careless, and I don’t want anyone thinking I’m dishonest.\n\nSo no, it doesn’t feel like a part of my personality or something I believe in. It feels more like something attacking me, honestly. I’ll be driving or trying to study, and it just keeps coming back when I’m trying to focus on what I’m actually supposed to be doing. I can’t make it shut up, even when another part of me is like, “You already checked. You know you checked.”\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about the safety checks are in line with your own values and beliefs?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my values are basically the opposite, I think. I do care about safety, obviously, and I don’t want anybody getting hurt in my cab. I want to be honest with my boss and do the check properly. That part is me.\n\nBut the thoughts themselves don’t feel like they’re “in line” with me. They feel like they take that normal thing — like wanting to be safe — and twist it until it’s all I can think about. Even when I know I did the safety check, this thought just keeps hitting me, like, “You missed something,” or “Somebody could get hurt because of you,” or “Your boss is going to think you lied about checking it.” And I can tell myself, “No, I checked it,” but it doesn’t really shut it up.\n\nIt’s especially bad because it follows me after the check too. Like I’ll be driving or trying to study, and instead of focusing, my mind keeps going back to the cab and whether I missed something. That’s not how I want to be. I don’t want to be stuck replaying the same fear over and over. So… no, I’d say the safety part lines up with my values, but the thoughts and the way they keep attacking me don’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about the safety checks are something you would normally think about, or do they feel out of character for you?\n\nPatient: No, it feels really out of character for me. I mean, I would normally care about doing the safety check right, because that’s part of the job and I don’t want to be irresponsible. But this is different. It’s like even after I know I did it, and I can remember doing it, the thought still comes back hard, like, “What if you missed something on the cab?” or “What if somebody gets hurt because of you?” And then it goes to, “What if your boss thinks you lied when you said you checked it?”\n\nThat’s not how I normally think. I don’t usually sit there doubting myself over and over like that. Or at least I didn’t used to. It feels like my brain is grabbing onto this one thing and won’t let go. I’ll be driving or trying to study, and I’m not even trying to think about the cab anymore, but it just pops back in and I can’t make it shut up.\n\nSo yeah, it feels out of character. The caring about safety part is normal for me, but the way the thought keeps attacking me after I’ve already checked… that doesn’t feel like me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to repeat any specific actions or rituals in response to these intrusive thoughts about missing something in your safety checks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. That’s probably the part that’s been getting the most out of control.\n\nBefore I can take a ride, I don’t just do the check once and move on. I’ll go around the car and check the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, the paperwork… and then I’ll finish, and almost immediately it feels like I have to do it again. Like if I don’t repeat it, then I’m just leaving something dangerous there on purpose, even though I know that’s not really true.\n\nSometimes I’ll go around the whole cab multiple times. I’ll check a tire, then the lights, then the doors, then I’ll think, “Wait, did I actually look closely enough at that tire?” and then I start the whole thing over. Or if I get interrupted, or if something just doesn’t feel right, I’ll restart from the beginning, because it feels like the whole check doesn’t count anymore.\n\nI’ve even taken pictures sometimes, like of the tires or certain things, just so I can prove to myself I checked it. But then even with the pictures, I’ll zoom in and look again, or I’ll think maybe the picture didn’t show the right angle, so then I’m back outside checking again.\n\nAnd it’s embarrassing because this happens when passengers are waiting too. I’ll know I’m already late, or I’ll know someone is standing there expecting me to leave, and I’m still stuck going around the car again because my head is saying, “No, you missed something, you have to make sure.” It’s like I can’t let myself start the ride until the check feels right, and a lot of the time it just doesn’t.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts and checking rituals causing you significant distress or problems in your daily life, such as at work, with friends, or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s definitely causing problems. It’s not just annoying or like I’m being careful. It’s actually messing up my life.\n\nWork is the biggest one. I’ve lost rides because I can’t get on the road on time. Like I’ll have someone waiting or I’ll be supposed to start, and I’m still stuck doing the checks again, and by the time I’m finally ready, the ride is gone or I’m late enough that it becomes a problem. I’ve gotten warnings for it too, which is embarrassing, because I’m not trying to be lazy or avoid work. I’m trying to do the job right, but it ends up looking like I can’t manage myself.\n\nAnd then it eats up so much of the day. It’s like I think, “Okay, I’ll just check and go,” but then the checking takes way longer than it should, and everything after that gets pushed back. I’m supposed to be studying for my CDL, and I keep telling myself I’ll do it after work or when I get home, but by then I’m either behind because of all the delays, or I’m exhausted from trying to catch up. So the studying just keeps getting pushed back and pushed back.\n\nAnd friends… yeah, I barely see them anymore. Not because I don’t want to, but because I’m always either behind on work, or trying to make up for lost time, or just stuck dealing with the fallout from being delayed. If someone asks me to hang out, I’m usually thinking, “I can’t, I’m already behind,” or I feel like I don’t have time because so much of the day got swallowed by the checks.\n\nSo yeah, it’s causing a lot of stress. It feels like this one thing is taking over way more of my life than it should.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, it is. I mean, outside of just the actual job part, it still follows me around, if that makes sense. Like because I lose so much time getting stuck in the checking, the rest of the day turns into me trying to catch up from being behind.

I’ve lost rides and I’ve gotten warnings because I can’t get on the road when I’m supposed to, and then after that I’m stressed and trying to make up for it. And then stuff I’m supposed to do outside of work, like studying for my CDL, keeps getting pushed back. I’ll tell myself, “Okay, I’ll study tonight,” but by the time I’m done dealing with delays or trying to catch up, I’m tired or it’s too late, and then it just doesn’t happen. So it’s not like the checking only takes a few minutes and then I’m fine — it kind of messes up the whole schedule.

And with friends, yeah… I barely see them anymore. Not because I don’t care about them or don’t want to hang out, but because I’m always behind on work or feeling like I have to catch up on everything I lost time on. If someone asks me to do something, I usually feel like I can’t, because I’m already trying to recover from the day getting eaten up by all the checking. It makes me feel pretty isolated, honestly, and embarrassed, because it sounds dumb when I try to explain why I’m never available.

Like, even when I know I did the safety check on my cab — I’ll remember doing it, or at least I think I remember doing it — this thought just hits me that maybe I missed something. Like maybe there’s something wrong with the cab and someone could get hurt, or my boss will think I lied about checking it.\n\nAnd it’s not just a quick worry and then it goes away. It keeps coming back while I’m driving, and then I’m trying to focus on the road but in my head I’m going over it again and again, like, “Did I really check that? What if I skipped something?” It makes it hard to drive normally, and even when I’m trying to study it’s there too, like I can’t shut it up. It’s exhausting, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel the need to perform specific actions or rituals to try and get rid of these thoughts about potentially missing something in your safety checks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I do. It’s kind of embarrassing because it starts as a normal safety check, like what I’m supposed to do before taking a ride, but then I can’t stop at just doing it once.\n\nI’ll go around the cab and check the tires, then the lights, then the doors, then the meter, then the paperwork, and I’ll think, “Okay, that’s done.” But then as soon as I’m about to let someone in or start driving, I get this feeling like it didn’t count, or like maybe I only looked but didn’t really check properly. So I go around again. And sometimes I’ll take pictures of things, like to prove to myself I checked them, but then even the pictures don’t always help because I’ll think maybe the picture was from before or maybe I didn’t check the right angle or something.\n\nAnd if it doesn’t feel right — like if I get interrupted, or I’m not completely sure in my head — I’ll restart the whole thing from the beginning. Tires, lights, doors, meter, paperwork, all over again. It can happen even when passengers are standing there waiting, which makes me feel terrible, and sometimes I’m already late but I still can’t just leave it. It’s like I need the check to feel “complete,” not just be complete.\n\nDoctor: Are these repeated checks you're doing intended to prevent something bad from happening, like an accident?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s what it feels like. Like I know the checks are supposed to prevent something bad, but I take it way past the normal amount. Before I can take a ride, I keep going around the car checking the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, the paperwork — and then even after I’ve done all of that, I’ll think, “What if I missed one thing and someone gets hurt?”\n\nSo I do it again. And sometimes again. And if it doesn’t feel right in my head, like if I wasn’t paying attention perfectly or someone distracted me, I’ll start the whole check over from the beginning. I’ve even taken pictures to try to prove to myself I checked, but then I start doubting that too.\n\nAnd it happens even when passengers are waiting there and I’m already late, which makes me feel awful, but I still feel like I can’t just drive off unless I’m sure. It’s like I’m trying to stop an accident or stop myself from being blamed for one, even if part of me knows I already checked.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that you fear might happen if you don't complete these checks perfectly?\n\nPatient: Yeah… the main thing is that I’ll miss something on the cab and then somebody gets hurt because of it. Like maybe I didn’t really check a tire properly, or a light, or something with the doors, and then I drive off and something happens. Even if I remember doing the check, there’s this thought that comes in like, “But what if you only think you did it? What if you skipped the important part?”\n\nAnd then there’s also this fear that if something did happen, my boss would say I lied about checking it, or that I was careless. Like I’d be responsible because I didn’t do it perfectly enough. That’s the part that keeps replaying in my head while I’m driving. I’m supposed to be focused, obviously, but instead I’m thinking back over the check again and again, trying to make sure I didn’t miss anything.\n\nIt’s not like I want to think about it. I know I did the check, or at least I’ll have this memory of doing it, but it doesn’t settle. The fear is basically that if I don’t get it completely right, someone could get hurt and it would be my fault, or people would think I lied about doing my job.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts and checking rituals affecting your relationships with friends, family, or colleagues?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has, definitely. Mostly with work, honestly, but that kind of spills into everything else. I’ve lost rides because I couldn’t get on the road fast enough. Like someone will be waiting, or I’m supposed to be ready, and I’m still going around the cab checking things over and over, and by the time I’m done, the ride is gone or they’re upset. I’ve gotten warnings for being late or not moving when I’m supposed to, which is… humiliating, because I’m not trying to be lazy or careless. It’s actually the opposite, but it doesn’t look that way from the outside.\n\nWith colleagues, it’s awkward because people notice. They’ll be like, “Are you still checking?” or they’ll get annoyed if I’m holding things up. I don’t really know how to explain it without sounding ridiculous, so I usually just say I’m making sure everything’s okay, but I can tell they think I’m overdoing it.\n\nAnd friends… I barely see them now. It’s not like I don’t want to. It’s just that so much of my day gets eaten up by the checking, and then I’m behind on work, and then I’m trying to catch up afterward. So if someone asks me to hang out, I’m either late, exhausted, or I feel like I can’t because I still have things I was supposed to do.\n\nEven my studying for my CDL keeps getting pushed back. I keep telling myself I’ll study after work, but then the day gets messed up from all the delays, and by the time I get home I’m just trying to recover or catch up. So yeah, it’s affecting a lot more than just the actual checking. It’s like everything gets backed up behind it.\n\nDoctor: Are you having these repetitive, intrusive thoughts about anything else besides the safety checks on your cab?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s that same cab safety thing. Like, it follows me around more than I’d expect. It’s not just when I’m standing there doing the check — it keeps coming back after I’ve already finished, even when I know I did it.\n\nI’ll be driving and this thought just hits me like, “What if you missed something? What if a tire wasn’t right, or the door wasn’t secure, and somebody gets hurt?” And then it turns into, “What if my boss thinks I lied about checking it?” or that I said I did it but didn’t really do it properly. I keep replaying it in my head, trying to prove to myself that I did everything, but it doesn’t really shut off.\n\nAnd it happens when I’m trying to study too. Like I’ll sit down and try to focus, and my mind goes right back to the cab — did I check this, did I miss that, what if something happens because of me. So… besides the safety checks, not really some whole separate topic, but the thoughts about the checks keep intruding into everything else.\n\nDoctor: Are you having these kinds of intrusive thoughts about anything else in your life besides the cab safety checks?\n\nPatient: Not really anything totally separate, no. It’s mainly the cab checks, but it doesn’t stay at the cab, if that makes sense. Like even when I know I did the safety check, the thought keeps coming back anyway — that I missed something, or I only think I checked it, and then somebody could get hurt because of me.\n\nAnd then it kind of branches into this thing where I picture my boss saying I lied, like I said I checked it but I didn’t really do it right. That part really gets stuck in my head. I’ll be driving and trying to focus on the road, and it’s still there in the background, like, “What if you missed it? What if something happens?” Or I’ll try to study, and instead of paying attention I’m going back over the check in my mind again.\n\nSo it’s not like I have a bunch of different fears about different parts of my life. It’s mostly that one thing, but it follows me into everything and I can’t really make it shut up.\n\nDoctor: Are your intrusive thoughts more about preventing something bad from happening, or are they also about what others might think of you if something goes wrong?\n\nPatient: It’s both, honestly. The biggest part is definitely trying to stop something bad from happening. Like, even after I know I did the safety check, my mind keeps hitting me with, “What if you missed something on the cab?” And then it goes straight to somebody getting hurt because I didn’t catch it. That’s the part that scares me the most.\n\nBut it also turns into what people would think, especially my boss. I get stuck on this idea that if something went wrong, he’d say I lied — like I claimed I checked it but didn’t really do it right. And I know that sounds kind of specific, but it just repeats in my head. It’s not like I can think, “Okay, I checked it, I’m done.” It keeps coming back while I’m driving, or when I’m trying to study, and I’ll be sitting there trying to focus but my brain is still back at the cab, replaying whether I missed something.\n\nSo yeah… it’s mainly about preventing someone from getting hurt, but then right behind that is this fear that people will think I was careless or dishonest. I can’t really make either part shut up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about missing something in your safety checks are a part of you, or do they feel like they're coming from outside of your own thoughts?\n\nPatient: They feel like they’re coming from my own head, I guess… not like a voice outside me or like somebody’s putting them there. It’s more like my own thoughts turn against me, if that makes sense.\n\nLike I’ll know I did the safety check. I can even remember doing it. But then this thought keeps hitting me anyway, like, “What if you missed something on the cab?” And it doesn’t feel like I chose to think that. It just pops back in, over and over, and then I’m stuck thinking somebody could get hurt or my boss could say I lied about checking it.\n\nSo it’s part of my thoughts, but it doesn’t feel like the normal kind of thinking where I can just decide to stop. It’s in my own mind, but it feels unwanted and it won’t shut up, especially when I’m driving or trying to study.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about missing something in your safety checks are something you want to think about, or do they feel unwanted?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t want to think about them at all. That’s kind of the whole problem. I’ll do the safety check and I’ll know, like logically, that I did it, but then the thought just comes right back anyway — like, what if I missed something on the cab, what if there’s something wrong and somebody gets hurt because of me.\n\nIt feels really unwanted, like it keeps forcing itself back into my head. I’m not sitting there trying to worry about it or analyze it on purpose. I’ll be driving and I need to be paying attention, or I’ll be trying to study, and it’s still there, replaying the check or making me doubt whether I really did it right. And then it turns into, what if my boss thinks I lied and says I didn’t actually check it properly.\n\nSo yeah, it’s definitely not something I want. I’ve tried to just tell myself, “You checked it, stop thinking about it,” but it doesn’t really shut up.\n\nDoctor: How distressed do you feel when these unwanted thoughts about missing something in your safety checks occur?\n\nPatient: Really distressed. Like… it doesn’t just feel like a normal worry where I can go, “Okay, I checked it, move on.” It hits me hard because the thought is always that I missed something on the cab and somebody could get hurt because of me. And even if part of me knows I did the safety check, the feeling doesn’t match that. It’s like my chest tightens and my brain just keeps circling back to it.\n\nAnd then I start thinking, what if my boss says I lied, like I said I checked it but I didn’t really do it right. That part makes it worse, because then it’s not just someone getting hurt, it’s also me being seen as careless or dishonest. I can’t really calm down from it easily.\n\nIt happens while I’m driving, which is awful because I’m supposed to be focused, and it happens when I’m trying to study too. I’ll be sitting there trying to read something and I’m still mentally back at the cab, replaying the safety check. So yeah, it’s very distressing. It feels like I can’t make it shut up, even when I know I already checked.\n\nDoctor: Are your repeated safety checks on the cab primarily done to prevent a specific bad event from happening?\n\nPatient: Yes… mostly, yeah. In my head it’s like I’m trying to stop something bad from happening before I take a ride. Like before I can actually leave, I keep going around the cab checking everything — the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, the paperwork — and I don’t just check once. I’ll go around again and again because it doesn’t feel settled.\n\nSometimes I’ll even take pictures, like to prove to myself I checked it, but then I’ll look at the picture and still think, “What if I missed something outside the frame?” or “What if I checked it wrong?” And then if it doesn’t feel right, I restart the whole check from the beginning, which is really frustrating because passengers can be waiting and I’m already late.\n\nSo yeah, it’s not just checking to be careful. It feels like if I don’t do it right, something could go wrong during the ride, and it would be my fault.\n\nDoctor: Are there any areas of your life where you feel like these thoughts and checking rituals are holding you back or causing problems that you hadn't mentioned yet?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s definitely causing problems in a lot of places now. Work is the biggest one, I guess, because if I can’t finish checking the cab and feel okay enough to leave, I just don’t get on the road on time. And then I’ve lost rides because of that. Like I’ll be supposed to pick someone up or start taking fares, and I’m still standing there going around the car again, or looking back at something I already checked, and by the time I’m ready it’s too late.\n\nI’ve gotten warnings for it too, because from the outside it probably just looks like I’m being slow or not managing my time, but it’s not like I’m just messing around. I’m stuck. I know I need to go, and I’m watching the time, and that makes me panic more, but then the checking gets worse because I’m thinking, “If I rush, that’s when I’ll miss something.” So it kind of traps me.\n\nAnd my CDL studying keeps getting pushed back. I’m trying to study and move forward, but so much of my day gets eaten up by the checking and then being behind afterward. By the time I actually sit down to study, I’m tired or I’m still thinking about whether I checked something right earlier. So it’s not just five minutes here and there — it takes over way more of the day than I want to admit.\n\nI barely see my friends anymore too. Not because I don’t want to, but because I’m either behind on work from the delays, or I’m trying to catch up on studying or other stuff I didn’t get done because I lost all that time checking. And then if someone asks me to hang out, I’m like, “I can’t, I’m behind,” but I’m always behind now. It’s embarrassing. It feels like my whole schedule is being controlled by this one thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts and behaviors are a part of who you are, or do they feel alien to you?\n\nPatient: They feel alien to me. I mean… it’s weird, because it’s my own brain saying it, so in that sense it feels like it’s coming from me, but it doesn’t feel like who I am or what I want to be thinking about all day.\n\nLike, I know I did the safety check. I can remember doing it. But then this thought keeps hitting me anyway, like, “What if you missed something on the cab?” or “What if somebody gets hurt because you didn’t catch it?” And then it turns into, “What if your boss thinks you lied when you said you checked it?” That part really bothers me, because I don’t want to be careless, and I don’t want anyone thinking I’m dishonest.\n\nSo no, it doesn’t feel like a part of my personality or something I believe in. It feels more like something attacking me, honestly. I’ll be driving or trying to study, and it just keeps coming back when I’m trying to focus on what I’m actually supposed to be doing. I can’t make it shut up, even when another part of me is like, “You already checked. You know you checked.”\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about the safety checks are in line with your own values and beliefs?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my values are basically the opposite, I think. I do care about safety, obviously, and I don’t want anybody getting hurt in my cab. I want to be honest with my boss and do the check properly. That part is me.\n\nBut the thoughts themselves don’t feel like they’re “in line” with me. They feel like they take that normal thing — like wanting to be safe — and twist it until it’s all I can think about. Even when I know I did the safety check, this thought just keeps hitting me, like, “You missed something,” or “Somebody could get hurt because of you,” or “Your boss is going to think you lied about checking it.” And I can tell myself, “No, I checked it,” but it doesn’t really shut it up.\n\nIt’s especially bad because it follows me after the check too. Like I’ll be driving or trying to study, and instead of focusing, my mind keeps going back to the cab and whether I missed something. That’s not how I want to be. I don’t want to be stuck replaying the same fear over and over. So… no, I’d say the safety part lines up with my values, but the thoughts and the way they keep attacking me don’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about the safety checks are something you would normally think about, or do they feel out of character for you?\n\nPatient: No, it feels really out of character for me. I mean, I would normally care about doing the safety check right, because that’s part of the job and I don’t want to be irresponsible. But this is different. It’s like even after I know I did it, and I can remember doing it, the thought still comes back hard, like, “What if you missed something on the cab?” or “What if somebody gets hurt because of you?” And then it goes to, “What if your boss thinks you lied when you said you checked it?”\n\nThat’s not how I normally think. I don’t usually sit there doubting myself over and over like that. Or at least I didn’t used to. It feels like my brain is grabbing onto this one thing and won’t let go. I’ll be driving or trying to study, and I’m not even trying to think about the cab anymore, but it just pops back in and I can’t make it shut up.\n\nSo yeah, it feels out of character. The caring about safety part is normal for me, but the way the thought keeps attacking me after I’ve already checked… that doesn’t feel like me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to repeat any specific actions or rituals in response to these intrusive thoughts about missing something in your safety checks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. That’s probably the part that’s been getting the most out of control.\n\nBefore I can take a ride, I don’t just do the check once and move on. I’ll go around the car and check the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, the paperwork… and then I’ll finish, and almost immediately it feels like I have to do it again. Like if I don’t repeat it, then I’m just leaving something dangerous there on purpose, even though I know that’s not really true.\n\nSometimes I’ll go around the whole cab multiple times. I’ll check a tire, then the lights, then the doors, then I’ll think, “Wait, did I actually look closely enough at that tire?” and then I start the whole thing over. Or if I get interrupted, or if something just doesn’t feel right, I’ll restart from the beginning, because it feels like the whole check doesn’t count anymore.\n\nI’ve even taken pictures sometimes, like of the tires or certain things, just so I can prove to myself I checked it. But then even with the pictures, I’ll zoom in and look again, or I’ll think maybe the picture didn’t show the right angle, so then I’m back outside checking again.\n\nAnd it’s embarrassing because this happens when passengers are waiting too. I’ll know I’m already late, or I’ll know someone is standing there expecting me to leave, and I’m still stuck going around the car again because my head is saying, “No, you missed something, you have to make sure.” It’s like I can’t let myself start the ride until the check feels right, and a lot of the time it just doesn’t.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts and checking rituals causing you significant distress or problems in your daily life, such as at work, with friends, or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s definitely causing problems. It’s not just annoying or like I’m being careful. It’s actually messing up my life.\n\nWork is the biggest one. I’ve lost rides because I can’t get on the road on time. Like I’ll have someone waiting or I’ll be supposed to start, and I’m still stuck doing the checks again, and by the time I’m finally ready, the ride is gone or I’m late enough that it becomes a problem. I’ve gotten warnings for it too, which is embarrassing, because I’m not trying to be lazy or avoid work. I’m trying to do the job right, but it ends up looking like I can’t manage myself.\n\nAnd then it eats up so much of the day. It’s like I think, “Okay, I’ll just check and go,” but then the checking takes way longer than it should, and everything after that gets pushed back. I’m supposed to be studying for my CDL, and I keep telling myself I’ll do it after work or when I get home, but by then I’m either behind because of all the delays, or I’m exhausted from trying to catch up. So the studying just keeps getting pushed back and pushed back.\n\nAnd friends… yeah, I barely see them anymore. Not because I don’t want to, but because I’m always either behind on work, or trying to make up for lost time, or just stuck dealing with the fallout from being delayed. If someone asks me to hang out, I’m usually thinking, “I can’t, I’m already behind,” or I feel like I don’t have time because so much of the day got swallowed by the checks.\n\nSo yeah, it’s causing a lot of stress. It feels like this one thing is taking over way more of my life than it should.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts and checking rituals interfering with your ability to maintain relationships or perform daily tasks outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it is. I mean, outside of just the actual job part, it still follows me around, if that makes sense. Like because I lose so much time getting stuck in the checking, the rest of the day turns into me trying to catch up from being behind.\n\nI’ve lost rides and I’ve gotten warnings because I can’t get on the road when I’m supposed to, and then after that I’m stressed and trying to make up for it. And then stuff I’m supposed to do outside of work, like studying for my CDL, keeps getting pushed back. I’ll tell myself, “Okay, I’ll study tonight,” but by the time I’m done dealing with delays or trying to catch up, I’m tired or it’s too late, and then it just doesn’t happen. So it’s not like the checking only takes a few minutes and then I’m fine — it kind of messes up the whole schedule.\n\nAnd with friends, yeah… I barely see them anymore. Not because I don’t care about them or don’t want to hang out, but because I’m always behind on work or feeling like I have to catch up on everything I lost time on. If someone asks me to do something, I usually feel like I can’t, because I’m already trying to recover from the day getting eaten up by all the checking. It makes me feel pretty isolated, honestly, and embarrassed, because it sounds dumb when I try to explain why I’m never available.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah… honestly, it has. Like, a lot. It feels like my whole day gets built around whether I can get through the checking or not, and most days it doesn’t feel like I’m in control of that.
